2018 · Question 3

The cytoplasm of the cell is considered a very important component because it

  1. regulates the amount of energy in the cell
  2. suspends cytoplasmic organelles
  3. is the outermost part of the cell
  4. is solely responsible for cell division
Answer and explanation

B: suspends cytoplasmic organelles

Cytoplasmic organelles are suspended within the cytoplasm. The cell membrane forms the outer boundary, and cell division involves several coordinated cellular structures.

2018 · Question 4

Enable JavaScript to verify this question's diagram before viewing its answer.

With a membrane permeable to water but not sugar, the liquid level in the thistle funnel will initially

  1. rise
  2. fall
  3. remain the same
  4. double
Answer and explanation

A: rise

Water moves from the distilled water into the sugar solution through the selectively permeable membrane. The added water pushes the solution higher in the narrow funnel stem.

2018 · Question 5

Enable JavaScript to verify this question's diagram before viewing its answer.

The illustrated experiment with a selectively permeable membrane demonstrates

  1. transportation
  2. water culture
  3. diffusion
  4. Osmosis
Answer and explanation

D: Osmosis

The apparatus demonstrates osmosis: net water movement through a selectively permeable membrane toward the sugar solution. The membrane separates solutions with different water potentials.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 7

Red blood cells burst after being placed in distilled water. This rupture is called

  1. plasmolysis
  2. diffusion
  3. haemolysis
  4. wilting
Answer and explanation

C: haemolysis

Red blood cells take up water in a strongly hypotonic solution such as distilled water. If they rupture, the breakdown of the red cells is called haemolysis.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 8

The curvature movement of plants in response to the stimulus of water is called

  1. hydrotropism
  2. geotropism
  3. Phototropism
  4. thigmotropism
Answer and explanation

A: hydrotropism

Hydrotropism is directional plant growth in response to a water gradient. Roots can grow toward wetter conditions, helping the plant obtain water.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 10

The longest bone in the adult human body is the

  1. humerus
  2. femur
  3. scapula
  4. tibia
Answer and explanation

B: femur

The femur, or thigh bone, is the longest bone in the normal adult human skeleton. It extends between the hip and knee and supports substantial body weight.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 11

Which of the following structures is not a skeletal material?

  1. Chitin
  2. Cartilage
  3. Bone
  4. Muscle
Answer and explanation

D: Muscle

Bone and cartilage form supporting skeletal tissues in vertebrates, while chitin contributes to many arthropod exoskeletons. Muscle contracts to move supporting structures.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 12

The slow flow of blood through capillary beds is useful because it helps

  1. because the walls of capillaries are very thin
  2. to avoid high — blood pressure
  3. to ensure that the individual does not get dizzy
  4. to allow adequate time for exchange of materials
Answer and explanation

D: to allow adequate time for exchange of materials

Slow capillary flow gives more time for exchange of gases, nutrients and wastes across vessel walls. The large total cross-sectional area of capillary beds helps produce that low velocity.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 13

Which of the following groups of organisms has kidney as their excretory organ?

  1. Fishes, amphibians, birds, man
  2. Fishes, amphibians, annelids, insects
  3. Fishes, reptiles, birds, tapeworms
  4. Fishes protozoans, amphibians, man
Answer and explanation

A: Fishes, amphibians, birds, man

Fishes, amphibians, birds and humans are vertebrates that use kidneys for excretion and water–salt regulation. The other groups include organisms with different excretory structures.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 14

Which is NOT a characteristic of arteries in the adult mammalian circulation? Arteries

  1. possess valves at intervals throughout their length
  2. have thick muscular and elastic walls
  3. carry blood away from the heart
  4. transport oxygenated blood, except for the pulmonary arteries
Answer and explanation

A: possess valves at intervals throughout their length

Arteries carry blood away from the heart and have walls suited to pressure. They do not have repeated valves along their length like many limb veins.

  1. The bird's blood was always warmer than that of the lizard
  2. The body temperature of the bird varied less than that of the lizard during changes in environmental temperature
  3. The body temperature of the bird remained constant despite changes in environmental temperature
  4. The body temperature of the lizard was always close to that of the environmental temperature
Answer and explanation

B: The body temperature of the bird varied less than that of the lizard during changes in environmental temperature

The dashed bird line changes only slightly, whereas the lizard line changes substantially. The bird temperature is therefore less variable, but is not perfectly constant.

2018 · Question 16

Enable JavaScript to verify this question's diagram before viewing its answer.

Which term describes the substantial variation in the lizard body temperature shown?

  1. Homeostasis
  2. Homeothermy
  3. Poikilothermy
  4. Osmoregulation
Answer and explanation

C: Poikilothermy

Poikilothermy describes variation in body temperature. The substantial change shown by the lizard is consistent with poikilothermy, rather than nearly constant body temperature.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 17

Compared with plain water, an appropriately formulated isotonic saline infusion helps

  1. because salt is a preservative
  2. to prevent contamination of the body
  3. to maintain the composition of body fluids
  4. to increase the number of blood cells
Answer and explanation

C: to maintain the composition of body fluids

An appropriately formulated isotonic saline solution helps preserve extracellular osmotic conditions. Plain water introduced into the circulation can cause harmful osmotic shifts in blood cells.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 18

The part of the inner ear containing sensory hair cells for hearing is the

  1. cochlea
  2. ampulla
  3. tympanum
  4. malleus
Answer and explanation

A: cochlea

The cochlea contains the organ of Corti, whose sensory hair cells respond to mechanical vibrations. They convert movement into signals transmitted through auditory nerve fibres.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 19

Spectacles with convex lenses correct long-sightedness by

  1. converging the Light rays before they enter the eye
  2. diverging the light rays before they enter the eye
  3. reducing light intensity before it enters the eye
  4. increasing light intensity before it enters the eye
Answer and explanation

A: converging the Light rays before they enter the eye

A convex lens adds convergence to incoming light. In a long-sighted eye this helps bring the focus forward onto the retina rather than behind it.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 21

Which of the following processes removes carbon from the atmosphere?

  1. Putrefaction
  2. Photosynthesis
  3. volcanic eruption
  4. Burning fuels
Answer and explanation

B: Photosynthesis

Photosynthesis takes carbon dioxide from the surroundings and incorporates its carbon into organic compounds. Combustion and decomposition generally release carbon dioxide instead.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 22

Which of the following cycles involves the process of precipitation and transpiration?

  1. Water cycles
  2. Carbon cycle
  3. Nitrogen cycle
  4. oxygen cycle
Answer and explanation

A: Water cycles

Precipitation returns atmospheric water to the surface, while transpiration releases water vapour from plants. Both are transfers within the water cycle.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 23

What is the critical limiting factor for plants below the photic zone in an aquatic ecosystem?

  1. Availability of nutrients
  2. Availability of water
  3. intensity of light
  4. Carbon dioxide concentration
Answer and explanation

C: intensity of light

Below the photic zone, insufficient light limits photosynthesis. Water may be abundant, but photosynthetic organisms still need enough usable light energy.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 24

Which instrument is used in sampling to estimate the number of plants in a habitat?

  1. Pooter
  2. Pitfall trap
  3. Quadrat
  4. Sweep net
Answer and explanation

C: Quadrat

A quadrat defines a known sample area in which plants can be counted. Representative repeated samples can then estimate abundance across a habitat.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 25

Which of the following statements is true about sandy soil? It

  1. has limited air space
  2. is light and easy to dig
  3. drains slowly
  4. is heavy and poorly aerated
Answer and explanation

B: is light and easy to dig

Sandy soil is generally relatively loose and easy to work. Its large pores commonly promote drainage and aeration, though organic matter and compaction can modify its behaviour.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 26

Which organism is a primary consumer when feeding on grass?

  1. Dog
  2. Sheep
  3. Grass
  4. Fungus
Answer and explanation

B: Sheep

A sheep feeding on plants is a primary consumer because it obtains energy directly from producers. Grass is a producer, while fungi commonly act as decomposers.

2018 · Question 27

In the food chain P → Q → R → S → T, P is a photosynthetic producer. Its initial energy source is

  1. sunlight
  2. carbohydrates
  3. green plants
  4. mineral salts
Answer and explanation

A: sunlight

In a grazing food chain beginning with a photosynthetic producer, sunlight supplies the initial energy. The producer converts some light energy into chemical energy passed along the chain.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 28

In P → Q → R → S → T, with P a producer and arrows showing energy flow, R

  1. feeds on S
  2. is a primary consumer
  3. is a producer as well as a consumer
  4. is a secondary consumer
Answer and explanation

D: is a secondary consumer

P is the producer and Q is the primary consumer. R feeds on Q, so R occupies the secondary-consumer level in this chain.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2018 · Question 29

Which condition is caused by a mite infestation rather than an inherited gene variant?

  1. Albinism
  2. Scabies
  3. Haemophilia
  4. Colour blindness
Answer and explanation

B: Scabies

Scabies is caused by infestation with the human itch mite. It is acquired through transmission of mites rather than inherited as a genetic condition.

2018 · Question 31

DNA in eukaryotic cells is contained in the

  1. central vacuole
  2. nucleus
  3. lysosome
  4. Golgi body
Answer and explanation

B: nucleus

The nucleus contains most of the DNA in a typical eukaryotic cell, organised into chromosomes. Mitochondria and chloroplasts can also contain DNA.

2018 · Question 33

Cytokinesis accompanying mitosis divides the cytoplasm so that

  1. daughter cells receive cytoplasm and organelles
  2. there is distribution of a complete set of genes into each daughter cell
  3. daughter cells inherit new genetic combinations
  4. worn out organelles are excluded from daughter cells
Answer and explanation

A: daughter cells receive cytoplasm and organelles

Cytokinesis divides the cytoplasm between daughter cells, distributing cytoplasmic contents and organelles. Chromosome segregation occurs during nuclear division.

2018 · Question 38

Which method can compare biological material from a crime scene with a reference sample to help identify its source?

  1. RNA analysis
  2. DNA analysis
  3. blood group test
  4. behavioural traits test
Answer and explanation

B: DNA analysis

DNA profiling compares genetic markers in biological samples. It can support identification when samples and statistical interpretation are reliable, but is one component of an investigation.

2018 · Question 40

The independently evolved similarity in swimming function and external form of whale flippers and fish fins illustrates

  1. divergent evolution
  2. coevolution
  3. continuous variation
  4. convergent evolution
Answer and explanation

D: convergent evolution

The swimming surfaces of whale flippers and fish fins show functional similarity associated with life in water. Such independently evolved similarities are examples of convergence.

2017 · Question 1

Piercing and sucking mouthparts for taking blood are found in female

  1. grasshoppers
  2. mosquitoes
  3. termites
  4. cockroaches
Answer and explanation

B: mosquitoes

Female mosquitoes have mouthparts adapted to pierce tissue and take a blood meal. The other listed insects have primarily biting and chewing mouthparts.

2017 · Question 4

Which group includes familiar animals adapted to arid environments?

  1. locust, camel, lizard and snakes
  2. monkeys, chameleon, earthworm and grasshopper
  3. monkeys, grasshopper, snail and snakes
  4. lungfish, duck, butterfly and lizards
Answer and explanation

A: locust, camel, lizard and snakes

Camels and many desert reptiles are adapted to dry conditions, and locusts include arid-region forms. The other groups include organisms strongly dependent on moist or aquatic environments.

2017 · Question 5

Suture joint is found in the

  1. hip
  2. ankle
  3. skull
  4. elbow
Answer and explanation

C: skull

Sutures are fibrous joints between many skull bones. They provide strong connections with little movement, unlike the freely movable joints of the limbs.

2017 · Question 6

The organelle responsible for osmoregulation in Paramecium is

  1. flame cell
  2. nephridia
  3. contractile vacuole
  4. Malpighian tubule
Answer and explanation

C: contractile vacuole

A contractile vacuole expels excess water entering a freshwater Paramecium. This helps maintain water balance in a cell whose surroundings are usually hypotonic.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 8

The platelets in mammalian blood are responsible for

  1. transporting oxygen
  2. initiating clotting
  3. removing carbon (IV) oxide
  4. destroying micro-organisms
Answer and explanation

B: initiating clotting

Platelets adhere at damaged blood vessels and help form a platelet plug. They also support clotting reactions that stabilise the plug with fibrin.

2017 · Question 10

When testing for the presence of starch in a leaf, the reason for dipping the decolourised leaf in hot water is to

  1. detect the starch
  2. kill the leaf
  3. soften the leaf
  4. remove the chlorophyll
Answer and explanation

C: soften the leaf

Alcohol removes chlorophyll but leaves the leaf brittle. Rinsing the decolourised leaf in hot water softens it before iodine is added to test for starch.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 11

If a remora benefits from transport by a shark while the shark is unaffected, the relationship is

  1. parasitism
  2. amensalism
  3. mutualism
  4. commensalism
Answer and explanation

D: commensalism

Commensalism describes a relationship in which one organism benefits while the other is not appreciably affected. A remora receiving transport under this assumption fits that definition.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 12

The major characteristic of a fresh water habitat is the possession of

  1. high turbidity
  2. high density
  3. low salinity
  4. high current
Answer and explanation

C: low salinity

Freshwater habitats have low concentrations of dissolved salts compared with marine habitats. Their turbidity and current speed vary between rivers, ponds and lakes.

2017 · Question 13

The causative organism of cholera is

  1. Clostridium sp
  2. shigella sp
  3. Vibrio cholerae
  4. Salmonella Typhi
Answer and explanation

C: Vibrio cholerae

Cholera is caused by toxigenic strains of Vibrio cholerae. Infection can produce severe watery diarrhoea through the action of cholera toxin in the intestine.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 14

The process that takes place in the dark stage of photosynthesis is

  1. oxidation of water
  2. photolysis of water
  3. oxidation of carbon (IV) oxide
  4. reduction of carbon (IV) oxide
Answer and explanation

D: reduction of carbon (IV) oxide

In the Calvin cycle, carbon derived from carbon dioxide is reduced using energy and reducing power supplied by the light reactions. The cycle does not require darkness.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 15

Historically, CFC refrigerants could escape into the atmosphere from

  1. crude oil refining
  2. coal mining
  3. motor vehicle exhaust
  4. cooling system
Answer and explanation

D: cooling system

Older refrigeration and air-conditioning systems used CFC refrigerants. Leakage or improper handling could release them into the atmosphere, where they contribute to ozone depletion.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 18

Which is a typical feature of wind-pollinated flowers?

  1. flowers lack nectar
  2. flowers are conspicuous
  3. flowers have perianths
  4. flowers are bisexual
Answer and explanation

A: flowers lack nectar

Wind-pollinated flowers typically do not offer nectar to animal visitors. They commonly have exposed anthers, receptive stigmas and abundant lightweight pollen.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 20

In the usual diploid animal life cycle, chromosome number is halved during gamete production by

  1. implantation
  2. fertilisation
  3. mitosis
  4. meiosis
Answer and explanation

D: meiosis

In animals, meiosis in the germ line reduces chromosome number during gamete production. Fertilisation then joins haploid gametes and restores the diploid number.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 21

A dish weighs 20 g and contains 7.5 g of dry soil. After burning, dish and soil weigh 25 g. Assuming the mass lost represents humus, its percentage in the initial dry soil is

  1. 9.1
  2. 37.5
  3. 12.5
  4. 33.3
Answer and explanation

D: 33.3

The remaining soil weighs 25 − 20 = 5 g. The mass lost is 7.5 − 5 = 2.5 g, so the estimated humus percentage is (2.5 ÷ 7.5) × 100 = 33.3%.

AI-reviewed · calculation checked

2017 · Question 25

The outermost tissue of a young herbaceous root is the

  1. cuticle
  2. pericycle
  3. epidermis
  4. endodermis
Answer and explanation

C: epidermis

The epidermis is the outer tissue of a young herbaceous root. Root hairs arise from epidermal cells and increase the surface available for absorption.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 26

The respective tissues that transport water and manufactured food in plants are

  1. xylem and phloem
  2. phloem and tracheid
  3. phloem and xylem
  4. xylem and tracheid
Answer and explanation

A: xylem and phloem

Xylem carries water and dissolved mineral nutrients, while phloem transports sugars and other organic solutes between sources and sinks. The order is xylem, then phloem.

AI-reviewed · source checked

2017 · Question 30

With other conditions comparable, which soil type generally has the least ability to retain nutrients?

  1. sandy loam
  2. clay loam
  3. loam
  4. sand
Answer and explanation

D: sand

Sandy soil generally has less surface area and lower nutrient retention than comparable soils containing more clay and organic matter. Dissolved nutrients can be readily leached.

2017 · Question 33

Overcrowding can encourage individuals to leave a population. This movement is

  1. immigration
  2. reduced competition
  3. emigration
  4. reduced mortality
Answer and explanation

C: emigration

Overcrowding can increase competition for resources and encourage individuals to leave an area. Movement out of a population is called emigration.

2017 · Question 34

The two specific vertebrae involved in nodding and rotating the head are the

  1. axis and cervical
  2. atlas and thoracis
  3. axis and atlas
  4. atlas and cervical
Answer and explanation

C: axis and atlas

The atlas supports the skull and participates in nodding, while the atlas–axis joint permits rotation around the axis dens. Together these are the first two cervical vertebrae.

  1. centrosome

  3. nucleus
  4. mitochondria
Answer and explanation

C: nucleus

Paternity comparisons commonly examine markers in nuclear DNA. An individual inherits nuclear genetic material from both biological parents, allowing marker comparisons.

2017 · Question 38

The modified insect-trapping structure of Venus flytrap leaves is an example of a

  1. adaptive coloration
  2. structural adaptation
  3. environmental adaptation
  4. behaviour adaptation
Answer and explanation

The Venus flytrap has leaves modified into traps that can retain insects. The shape and specialised parts of those leaves are structural adaptations for obtaining nutrients.

2017 · Question 39

Morphological variation in humans include

  1. height, skin, colour and tongue rolling
  2. weight, finger prints and body shape
  3. height, weight and blood group
  4. skin colour, blood and height
Answer and explanation

B: weight, finger prints and body shape

Weight, fingerprint patterns and body shape describe bodily characteristics. Blood group concerns biochemical antigen differences, while tongue rolling concerns an ability.

2016 · Question 1

Which of the following structures is a protective adaptive feature of the Agama Lizard to the environment?

  1. Nuchal crest
  2. Claws
  3. Scaly skin
  4. Gular fold
Answer and explanation

C: Scaly skin

Keratinised scales protect lizard skin and help limit water loss. This covering supports life on land, unlike the generally more permeable skin of amphibians.

2016 · Question 2

Which structure is directly specialised for processing or taking in food in an insect?

  1. suitable mouthparts
  2. paired antennae
  3. segmented body
  4. jointed appendages
Answer and explanation

A: suitable mouthparts

Insect mouthparts are modified for tasks such as biting, sucking or piercing food. Their form is closely related to how an insect obtains its diet.

2016 · Question 4

Which of the following is NOT part of the carbon cycle?

  1. Organic carbon
  2. Decomposition
  3. Nitrates formation
  4. Photosynthesis
Answer and explanation

C: Nitrates formation

Nitrate formation belongs primarily to the nitrogen cycle. Photosynthesis, decomposition and organic carbon storage are directly involved in movement of carbon through ecosystems.

2016 · Question 5

I. Tissues II. System III. Cell IV. Organs Which of the above is the level of organization of a leaf?

  1. IV
  2. I
  3. III
  4. II
Answer and explanation

A: IV

A leaf is an organ containing several tissues that work together. Epidermal, vascular and photosynthetic tissues contribute different functions within that one structure.

2016 · Question 6

In cellular respiration, energy is stored in the form of

  1. heat energy
  2. adenosine diphosphate
  3. adenosine monophosphate
  4. adenosine triphosphate
Answer and explanation

D: adenosine triphosphate

Cellular respiration transfers some energy from fuels into ATP, adenosine triphosphate. Cells then couple ATP reactions to processes requiring energy.

  1. root hair
  2. growing root
  3. mature fruit
  4. green leaf
Answer and explanation

D: green leaf

A green leaf is a major photosynthetic organ in a typical leafy plant. Its chloroplast-rich tissues capture light and produce organic compounds.

2016 · Question 9

The process by which heritable favourable traits become more common through differential survival and reproduction is

  1. gene mutation
  2. dispersal
  3. overcrowding
  4. natural selection
Answer and explanation

D: natural selection

Natural selection changes populations when heritable traits affect survival and reproduction. Individuals with advantageous variants can leave more descendants under the prevailing conditions.

2016 · Question 10

The rise and fall of ocean water during the day is referred to as

  1. gravity
  2. a pull
  3. tide
  4. zone
Answer and explanation

C: tide

Tides are periodic changes in sea level driven chiefly by the gravitational effects of the Moon and Sun together with Earth’s rotation. Local coasts affect their pattern.

2016 · Question 11

Which of the following is a producer in an aquatic habitat?

  1. Nymphaea
  2. Dryopteris
  3. planarian
  4. Simulium
Answer and explanation

A: Nymphaea

Nymphaea, a water lily, photosynthesises and acts as a producer in aquatic habitats. Planarians and blackflies are consumers rather than photosynthetic plants.

2016 · Question 13

I. Tissue II. System III. Cell IV Organ The correct sequence of increasing level of complexity is

  1. IV-II-III
  2. I-II-III-IV
  3. IV-III-I-II
  4. III-I-IV-II
Answer and explanation

D: III-I-IV-II

Cells form tissues, tissues form organs, and organs cooperate in organ systems. The sequence is therefore III, I, IV, II.

2016 · Question 17

Bacteria which add atmospheric nitrogen to the soil are

  1. putrefying bacteria
  2. nitrifying bacteria
  3. nitrogen fixing bacteria
  4. denitrifying bacteria
Answer and explanation

C: nitrogen fixing bacteria

Nitrogen-fixing bacteria convert atmospheric nitrogen into biologically usable nitrogen compounds. This introduces nitrogen into soil food webs rather than returning it to the atmosphere.

  1. Courtship
  2. defence
  3. water conservation
  4. obtaining food
Answer and explanation

B: defence

Hedgehog spines deter predators, especially when the animal curls to expose its spiny covering. They are modified hairs serving a protective function.

Which of the following instruments is used to measure temperature?

  1. Thermometer
  2. Hygrometer
  3. Anemometer
  4. Hydrometer
Answer and explanation

A: Thermometer

A thermometer measures temperature using a property that changes predictably with thermal conditions. It is different from instruments for humidity, wind or liquid density.

2016 · Question 21

In human, puffiness and water retention in the body is a possible symptom of

  1. bladder malfunction
  2. poor digestion
  3. kidney malfunction
  4. obesity
Answer and explanation

C: kidney malfunction

Kidney dysfunction can impair salt and water regulation and contribute to swelling. Swelling has several possible causes, so this symptom alone is not a diagnosis.

  1. Linnaeus
  2. Darwin
  3. Lamarck
  4. Mendel
Answer and explanation

B: Darwin

Darwin developed an account of descent with modification linking living forms through ancestry. Natural selection was a central proposed mechanism of evolutionary change.

2016 · Question 23

Dietary roughage in humans helps bowel contents move through the gut and can therefore

  1. provide energy
  2. slow down aging
  3. ease digestion
  4. prevent disease
Answer and explanation

C: ease digestion

Dietary fibre supports movement and bulk of intestinal contents in humans. Some fibre is fermented by gut microbes, but much resists digestion by human enzymes.

The lowest taxonomic rank among these choices is

  1. Kingdom
  2. class
  3. phylum
  4. species
Answer and explanation

D: species

Species is the lowest taxonomic rank among the listed categories. Kingdom, phylum and class are broader groupings containing many species.

2016 · Question 27

Two important process involved in the absorption and transport of materials in plants are

  1. flaccidity and turgidity
  2. diffusion and plasmolysis
  3. plasmolysis and capillarity
  4. osmosis and diffusion
Answer and explanation

D: osmosis and diffusion

Osmosis moves water across selectively permeable membranes, while diffusion moves substances down concentration gradients. Both contribute to material movement in plants.

2016 · Question 29

The organelle responsible for oxidative phosphorylation in a typical eukaryotic cell is the

  1. nucleus
  2. nucleolus
  3. endoplasmic reticulum
  4. mitochondrion
Answer and explanation

D: mitochondrion

Mitochondria are the sites of oxidative phosphorylation in typical eukaryotic cells. Glycolysis takes place in the cytosol, so cellular respiration is not exclusively mitochondrial.

2016 · Question 30

In which part of Nigeria are Mangrove swamps found?

  1. Chad Basin
  2. Niger Delta
  3. Benue Valley
  4. Mambilla Plateau
Answer and explanation

B: Niger Delta

The Niger Delta includes coastal tidal wetlands supporting extensive mangroves. Mangroves tolerate saline, waterlogged conditions unlike the inland highlands and basins listed.

2016 · Question 32

In a small unicellular organism, diffusion is sufficient for transport because

  1. the surface area to volume ratio is small
  2. they have lungs for diffusion
  3. materials have to move over long distance
  4. the surface area to volume ratio is large
Answer and explanation

D: the surface area to volume ratio is large

Small cells have a relatively large surface area compared with their volume and short internal transport distances. These features make exchange by diffusion more effective.

Lamarck’s historical proposal included inheritance of changes acquired during an individual’s life. In that account, increased use of muscles could enlarge them and the enlargement was proposed to pass to descendants.

Fungi lack chlorophyll and obtain organic carbon by absorbing materials from other organisms or their remains. Their heterotrophy is defined by this dependence on organic food, not by the shape of their bodies.

Sweat glands release water, salts and small quantities of nitrogenous substances onto the skin. Sweating is chiefly important for thermoregulation but also removes these substances from the body.

  2. Uric acid
  3. Sweat
  4. Mucilage
Answer and explanation

Many terrestrial insects excrete nitrogen chiefly as uric acid or urates. These poorly soluble products can be eliminated with relatively little water, which helps conserve water on land.

The vertebrate endoskeleton supports the body and protects organs, such as the brain within the skull. Muscles act on the skeleton to produce movement, while ligaments connect bones at joints.

Pollination is the transfer of pollen from an anther to a stigma in flowering plants. Fertilisation occurs later, when gametes fuse following pollen germination and pollen-tube growth.

Radial symmetry arranges body parts around a central axis so several planes through that axis divide the body into similar halves. Bilateral symmetry instead has one principal left-right plane.

Ultrafiltrate crosses the glomerular filtration barrier into the capsular space of Bowman’s capsule. This is the first stage of urine formation, before tubular reabsorption and secretion modify the filtrate.

At carrying capacity in the simple logistic model, population size is approximately stable because net population growth is zero. Growth slows before the population reaches that level.

In ordinary double-stranded DNA, adenine pairs with thymine and guanine pairs with cytosine. These complementary pairs help preserve the sequence relationship between the two strands during replication.

2011 · Question 12

Which of the following can cause shrinkage of living cells?

  1. Hypotonic solution
  2. Isotonic solution
  3. Deionized water
  4. Hypertonic solution
Answer and explanation

D: Hypertonic solution

A hypertonic solution draws water out of a cell by osmosis and can cause the cell contents to shrink. A hypotonic solution instead favours water entry, while an isotonic solution causes no net osmotic change.

  2. II and III only
  3. I only
  4. I, II and III

Dilute acid or the appropriate enzyme can hydrolyse sucrose into glucose and fructose. Water alone does not appreciably hydrolyse sucrose during the usual laboratory test period, so tubes II and III are positive.

  3. disease rate

Answer and explanation

C: disease rate

Crowding can increase contact between individuals and facilitate the spread of infectious agents. The outcome depends on the pathogen and conditions, but increased disease transmission is a common density-dependent effect.

2011 · Question 43

At a single flower-colour locus, crossing true-breeding red and white plants produces pink heterozygotes. This illustrates

  1. codominance
  2. incomplete dominance
  3. mutation
  4. linkage
Answer and explanation

B: incomplete dominance

Incomplete dominance produces a heterozygous phenotype intermediate between the two homozygotes, such as pink flowers from red and white parental lines. Both alleles remain distinct rather than permanently blending.

  4. inbreeding
Answer and explanation

A: cross-breeding

Cross-breeding combines individuals from genetically different lines or varieties within a species. It can combine useful traits, whereas inbreeding mates closely related individuals.

The usual ABO inheritance model has three alleles in the population: IA, IB and i. Each person normally carries two of these, producing the familiar A, B, AB or O phenotypes.

  1. convergent evolution
  2. continuous variation
  3. coevolution
  4. divergent evolution
Answer and explanation

A: convergent evolution

Bird and bat wings evolved independently as flight structures. Their underlying forelimbs are homologous through shared vertebrate ancestry, but the independent acquisition of wings for powered flight illustrates convergence.

  2. the nucleus is pushed to the centre of the cell

  4. the nucleus is small and irregular in shape
Answer and explanation

A typical plant cell has a cell wall containing cellulose. In many mature cells, a large central vacuole occupies much of the volume and displaces the cytoplasm and nucleus toward the edge.

In a typical dicot stem vascular bundle, xylem lies toward the centre and phloem toward the outside. Cambium is between them, and the bundles form a ring inside the outer tissues.

Systemic circulation supplies tissues through the aorta and its branches and returns blood to the right side of the heart. The pulmonary circuit carries blood between the heart and the lungs for gas exchange.

2010 · Question 46

  1. 2n → 2n
  2. n → n
  3. n → 2n
  4. 2n → n
Answer and explanation

D: 2n → n

Meiosis reduces the chromosome-set number from diploid, 2n, to haploid, n. DNA replicates before the first division, but replication does not itself double the number of chromosome sets.

2010 · Question 47

  1. 3⁄4
  2. 1⁄2
  3. 1⁄4
  4. 1
Answer and explanation

C: 1⁄4

For Aa × Aa, each parent produces A and a gametes equally. The four equally likely combinations are AA, Aa, aA and aa, giving a recessive aa phenotype in one quarter of offspring.

2004 · Question 2

Stomatal opening is promoted by

  1. presence of guard cells
  2. decrease in solute concentration in the guard cells
  3. increase in solute concentration in the guard cells
  4. presence of a pore
Answer and explanation

C: increase in solute concentration in the guard cells

An increase in guard-cell solute concentration lowers water potential. Water enters by osmosis, turgor rises and the guard cells bow apart, opening the pore.

  4. different eggs and sperms

2003 · Question 2

A frog is partly submerged in water. Arrow I points outwards from its exposed back into the air, and arrow II points from the surrounding water into its submerged skin. The processes at I and II respectively are

  1. evaporation and osmosis
  2. exhalation and osmosis
  3. osmosis and diffusion
  4. urination and diffusion
Answer and explanation

A: evaporation and osmosis

Water can evaporate from a frog’s moist skin exposed to air. Water can enter the submerged skin by osmosis when the surrounding water has a higher water potential than the body fluids.

  3. leaves are important to photosynthesis
  4. water is necessary for photosynthesis

Answer and explanation

C: xylem

Water absorbed by a plant moves upward mainly through xylem. A suitable dissolved dye can follow that water stream and stain the conducting xylem tissues.

  1. III
  2. IV
  3. II
  4. I
Answer and explanation

B: IV

The ratios correspond to 0.5, about 0.33, 0.25 and 0.20 units of surface area per unit volume. IV has the least exchange surface relative to its volume and therefore the greatest transport challenge on this criterion.

  4. any traits acquired by an organism during its lifetime can be passed on to its offspring
Answer and explanation

Natural selection favours inherited variants that improve reproductive success in the current environment. Individuals carrying favourable variants tend to leave more descendants, changing their frequency over generations.

  1. 1/5
  2. ¼
  3. 1/3
  4. ½
Answer and explanation

With independent births and a probability of one half for a girl in this model, the previous four outcomes do not change the fifth. The probability remains 1/2.

2002 · Question 50

At a single locus with complete dominance, what proportion of offspring from two heterozygous parents, Aa × Aa, is expected to express the recessive phenotype?

  1. ¼
  2. ½
  3. ¾
  4. 4/4
Answer and explanation

A: ¼

For Aa × Aa, the equally likely allele combinations are AA, Aa, aA and aa. With complete dominance, only aa expresses the recessive phenotype, giving a probability of 1/4.

  1. commensalism
  2. mycorrhiza
  3. parasitism
  4. symbiosis
Answer and explanation

D: symbiosis

Rhizobium bacteria in legume root nodules obtain carbon compounds and a suitable habitat from the plant. In return, nitrogen fixation supplies nitrogen compounds useful to the plant. This is a mutualistic symbiosis.

Which option most precisely describes the positions of a dominant allele and its recessive alternative in a heterozygous diploid cell?

  1. on different chromosomes in the cell
  2. at corresponding loci on a homologous chromosome pair
  3. mother’s sex cell
  4. mother’s X chromosome
Answer and explanation

B: at corresponding loci on a homologous chromosome pair

Alternative alleles of the same gene occupy corresponding loci on a homologous chromosome pair. In a heterozygote, one copy may be dominant and the other recessive.

2001 · Question 39

  1. father’s sex cell
  2. father’s somatic chromosome
  3. mother’s sex cell
  4. mother’s X chromosome
Answer and explanation

A: father’s sex cell

In the usual XX–XY inheritance pattern, the egg contributes an X chromosome. The fertilising sperm contributes either X or Y, producing an XX or XY chromosome complement respectively.

2001 · Question 40

  1. Group O is the universal recipient
  2. Group A can donate to group A only
  3. Group AB is the universal recipient
  4. Group B can donate to group B only
Answer and explanation

C: Group AB is the universal recipient

In the ABO model for red cells, group AB lacks anti-A and anti-B antibodies and can receive cells from any ABO group. Rh type, other antibodies and compatibility testing also matter in real transfusions.

  2. reptilia
  3. arthropoda
  4. Cnidaria (coelenterates)
Answer and explanation

D: Cnidaria (coelenterates)

Cnidarians have a digestive cavity but no true coelom lined by mesoderm. A digestive cavity is not a coelom. Molluscs and arthropods have a reduced coelom, while reptiles have a well-developed body cavity.

2000 · Question 20

  1. food storage must be low
  2. catabolism must exceed that of anabolism
  3. anabolism must exceed that of catabolism
  4. food storage must be high
Answer and explanation

C: anabolism must exceed that of catabolism

Anabolism builds complex substances, whereas catabolism breaks them down. Net accumulation of body material requires synthesis to exceed breakdown over the growth period.

2000 · Question 27

A dry, air-containing soil sample has bulk volume q cm³. Water initially occupies p cm³. After mixing, allowing trapped air to escape without loss of water or soil, the total mixture volume is r cm³. Which relation follows?

  1. r=p+q
  2. r>p+q
  3. q=r-p
  4. r<p+q
Answer and explanation

D: r<p+q

The initial soil volume includes air-filled pore spaces. When water enters those spaces and air escapes, the final mixture occupies less than the sum of the separately measured water and soil volumes.

  1. II and III
  2. I and III
  3. I and IV
  4. I and II
Answer and explanation

B: I and III

High births and immigration add individuals, while low mortality and emigration minimise losses. Combining I and III therefore most consistently favours rapid population increase.

  3. radial symmetry
  4. longitudinal section

Bilateral symmetry means that one longitudinal plane divides the body into matching left and right halves. A section is a cut through a body, whereas symmetry describes how its parts are arranged.

  4. increasing the breakdown of the protein portion of the erythrocytes

1999 · Question 20

The force that holds water together in the form of a stream within the xylem tube is the

  1. cohesion of water molecules to one another by hydrogen bonds
  2. force of gravity attracting the water molecules through the hydrogen bonds
  3. attraction between the water and xylem by osmotic force
  4. normal flow of water from the ground with the help of gravitational force
Answer and explanation

A: cohesion of water molecules to one another by hydrogen bonds

Hydrogen bonds between water molecules produce cohesion. This helps maintain a continuous column of water in xylem as transpiration creates tension. Attraction to the vessel walls is adhesion, a different interaction.

  1. predation
  2. commensalism
  3. parasitism
  4. competition
Answer and explanation

Commensalism describes an interaction in which one participant benefits while the other has no significant effect under the conditions considered. It is represented by the signs + and 0.

1999 · Question 41

A food web has these numbered organisms: 1 plant, 2 moth, 3 grasshopper, 4 praying mantis, 5 lizard, 6 predatory organism and 7 herbivorous bug. Arrows point from food to consumer: 1→2, 1→3, 1→7, 2→4, 2→5, 2→6, 3→4, 3→5, 3→6, 4→5, 6→5, 7→5 and 7→6. For the usual upright terrestrial biomass pattern, biomass is likely to increase in which sequence?

  1. 1 → 2 → 4 → 5
  2. 1 → 3 → 4 → 5
  3. 3 → 5 → 6 → 7
  4. 5 → 4 → 3 → 1
Answer and explanation

D: 5 → 4 → 3 → 1

In the usual upright biomass pattern for a terrestrial food chain, biomass tends to be greater toward the producer level. The feeding chain plant 1 → grasshopper 3 → praying mantis 4 → lizard 5 is therefore read in reverse for increasing biomass.

1999 · Question 43

If the offspring of a cross between brown mouse (bb) and a black mouse (BB) are allowed to interbreed, how many different genotypes would result?

  1. 2
  2. 3
  3. 4
  4. 5
Answer and explanation

B: 3

The first cross bb × BB produces Bb offspring. Crossing two of those offspring gives BB, Bb, bB and bb. Bb and bB are the same genotype, so there are three distinct genotypes: BB, Bb and bb.

From an evolutionary standpoint, the older a fossil- bearing rock is the more likely it is to contain

  1. aves as opposed to amphibians
  2. invertebrates as opposed to vertebrates
  3. angiosperms as opposed to algae
  4. vertebrates as opposed to invertebrates
Answer and explanation

B: invertebrates as opposed to vertebrates

Invertebrate animal lineages appear in the fossil record before vertebrates. Very old fossil-bearing deposits are therefore more likely to preserve invertebrates than vertebrates, although the actual fossils depend on the environment and preservation.

1999 · Question 48

Which of the following is one of Lamarck’s theories?

  2. all living organisms are constantly involved in a struggle for existence
  3. the size of a given population remains fairly constant
  4. new species originate through the inheritance of acquired traits
Answer and explanation

D: new species originate through the inheritance of acquired traits

Lamarck proposed that characteristics acquired during an organism’s lifetime could be inherited. This historical idea differs from natural selection acting on inherited variation across generations.

A hen’s alarm call can alert chicks and other chickens to a nearby predator. The response helps others take cover or remain vigilant and differs from a territorial advertisement.

  4. is located at the centre of the cell
Answer and explanation

A: contains the genetic material

The nucleus contains genetic information used to direct protein production and many cellular activities. Its regulatory role comes from that information and its expression, rather than simply its position or enclosing membrane.

  3. their bodies immersed in the nutrients
  4. their outer membrane made of cellulose
Answer and explanation

B: a large surface-area-to-volume ratio

A small unicellular organism has much surface area relative to its volume and short internal diffusion distances. These features help substances cross its surface and reach the cell interior without a large transport system.

The adult frog heart has two atria, also called auricles, and one ventricle. The atria receive blood from different circuits, while the single ventricle pumps blood into the outflow pathways.

  4. antibodies bind to their corresponding antigens on red blood cells
Answer and explanation

Agglutination can occur when antibodies bind to their corresponding antigens on red blood cells and link cells together. Compatibility matters because an antibody reacts with its specific target rather than with any different antigen.

The chicks have different body masses despite sharing several stated conditions. Differences among individuals of the same species are variation. A single set of masses does not establish their growth rates or the genetic cause of the differences.

  4. is diploid and independent of the gametophyte
Answer and explanation

The mature fern sporophyte is diploid and can live independently. It produces haploid spores by meiosis. The young sporophyte initially develops on the gametophyte before becoming an independent fern.

1997 · Question 19

Compared with aerobic respiration of glucose, alcoholic fermentation in yeast produces

  1. less amount of energy and water
  2. greater amount of energy and alcohol
  3. a smaller amount of usable energy and alcohol
  4. a greater amount of usable energy and water
Answer and explanation

C: a smaller amount of usable energy and alcohol

Alcoholic fermentation in yeast yields ethanol and much less usable energy per glucose than aerobic respiration. Only the small ATP yield of glycolysis is obtained, while fermentation regenerates NAD+ for glycolysis to continue.

Deamination removes nitrogen-containing amino groups from amino acids. In mammals, the resulting nitrogen can be incorporated into urea for excretion, preventing toxic ammonia from accumulating.

1997 · Question 44

Both parents have sickle cell trait, with genotype HbA/HbS. What is the probability that a child has genotype HbA/HbA?

  1. ½
  2. ¼
  3. 1/8
  4. 0
Answer and explanation

B: ¼

Each HbA/HbS parent can contribute HbA or HbS. The four equally likely combinations are HbA/HbA, HbA/HbS, HbS/HbA and HbS/HbS. Only one has no HbS allele, giving a probability of one quarter for each pregnancy.

  4. and the triceps muscle relax

  4. lower side of the shoot
Answer and explanation

In a young shoot exposed to light from one side, auxin becomes more concentrated on the shaded side. Greater elongation there bends the shoot toward the light, producing positive phototropism.

  2. olfactory lobe
  3. cerebellum
  4. medulla oblongata
Answer and explanation

C: cerebellum

The cerebellum helps coordinate movement, balance and timing, all of which are important for walking smoothly. Walking also involves the cerebral cortex, brainstem, spinal circuits and sensory feedback.

1995 · Question 32

In a food chain involving a primary producer, a primary consumer as well as a secondary consumer, the sharing of trophic energy is in the form that the

  1. primary consumer has more energy than the primary producer
  2. secondary consumer takes up all the energy contents of the primary consumer
  3. energy is shared equally between the three groups of organism
  4. secondary consumer gets only a small portion of the energy captured by the primary producer
Answer and explanation

D: secondary consumer gets only a small portion of the energy captured by the primary producer

Only part of the energy at one feeding level becomes available to the next. Organisms use energy in metabolism and release heat, and not all biomass is consumed or assimilated, so less reaches secondary consumers.

  3. turgidity
  4. haemolysis

Haemolysis is rupture of a red blood cell with release of its contents. Distilled water can cause this by entering the cell osmotically, but osmosis names the water movement rather than the final rupture.

  3. behind the retina
  4. on the blind spot
Answer and explanation

B: in front of the retina

In myopia, parallel rays from a distant object are brought to a focus in front of the retina when the eye is uncorrected. The light is therefore out of focus when it reaches the retinal surface.

1994 · Question 43

In a simplified autosomal model, brown allele B is dominant to blue allele b. A BB girl has a bb brother. What are their parents’ eye-colour phenotypes in this model?

  1. Both parents have blue eyes
  2. Their father has blue eyes and their mother has brown eyes
  3. Both parents have brown eyes
  4. Their mother has blue eyes and their father has brown eyes
Answer and explanation

C: Both parents have brown eyes

In the stated single-gene model, a BB child must receive B from each parent, while a bb child must receive b from each. Both parents must therefore be Bb and show the dominant brown-eye phenotype in this model.

  1. grasses
  2. conifers
  3. legumes
  4. palms

Conifers are gymnosperms and produce seeds not enclosed within an ovary-derived fruit. Grasses, legumes and palms are flowering plants, whose seeds develop within fruits. Naked does not mean lacking a seed coat.

1993 · Question 3

  1. Absence of flower
  2. Alternation of generation
  3. Dependence on water for reproduction
  4. Presence of a vascular system
Answer and explanation

D: Presence of a vascular system

Pteridophytes possess vascular tissues for water and food transport, while bryophytes lack a true xylem-and-phloem system. Both groups lack flowers and have alternating generations, and their motile sperm need water for fertilisation.

In a typical green fern life cycle, both the leafy sporophyte and the small gametophyte photosynthesise and contain chloroplasts. The gametophyte has rhizoids, while the sporophyte has true roots and leaves.

  3. hydrotropism

Geotropism, also called gravitropism, is directional growth in response to gravity. Roots usually grow toward gravity, while shoots grow away from it. Light, water and touch are different directional stimuli.

  4. constrict to increase the amount of blood flowing to the skin

  2. Eardrum → oval window → ossicles
  3. Eardrum → ossicles → oval window
  4. Ossicles → eardrum → oval window
Answer and explanation

C: Eardrum → ossicles → oval window

Sound causes the eardrum to vibrate. These vibrations pass through the middle-ear ossicles to the oval window, where they generate pressure waves in the fluid of the inner ear.

The pancreas supplies insulin, which helps regulate blood glucose by promoting its uptake and storage. Removing the pancreas without replacement insulin removes this control and can cause blood glucose to rise.

  3. combining with haemoglobin
  4. rupturing the blood vessels
Answer and explanation

C: combining with haemoglobin

Carbon monoxide binds to haemoglobin, forming carboxyhaemoglobin. This reduces effective oxygen transport and delivery to tissues. The resulting oxygen deprivation is a major mechanism of carbon monoxide toxicity.

AI-reviewed · source checked

  2. Tongue rolling
  3. Body weight
  4. Height

B: Tongue rolling

When tongue rolling is recorded simply as can roll or cannot roll, the observations fall into separate categories. Height and body weight vary across a range, and skin pigmentation is also a quantitative trait.

  2. 1 homozygous smooth : 2 heterozygous smooth : 1 wrinkled
  3. 2 homozygous smooth : 2 wrinkled
  4. 3 heterozygous smooth : 1 wrinkled
Answer and explanation

B: 1 homozygous smooth : 2 heterozygous smooth : 1 wrinkled

The four cells contain SS, Ss, sS and ss. Ss and sS are the same heterozygous genotype, giving one homozygous smooth, two heterozygous smooth and one homozygous wrinkled entry.

  2. homoiothermic
  3. polythermic
  4. homeostatic
Answer and explanation

A: poikilothermic

Poikilothermic describes an animal whose body temperature varies substantially. This differs from homeothermy, in which body temperature is relatively stable. Heat source and temperature variability are related but distinct classifications.

1992 · Question 1

Which organelle carries out major stages of aerobic cellular respiration?

  1. endoplasmic reticulum
  2. ribosome
  3. golgi body
  4. mitochondrion
Answer and explanation

D: mitochondrion

Mitochondria carry out major stages of aerobic cellular respiration and produce much of a cell’s ATP. Glycolysis occurs in the cytosol, so mitochondria are not the site of every reaction involved in respiration.

1992 · Question 3

Which of the following correctly summarizes the life cycle of a fern plant?

  1. Spore → prothallus → thallus → sporangium
  2. Male and female gametangia → zygospore → sporangium → spores
  3. Spore → thallus → spermatozoa + ovum → sporangium
  4. Prothallus → spermatozoid + egg cell → leafy plant → sporangium → spore
Answer and explanation

D: Prothallus → spermatozoid + egg cell → leafy plant → sporangium → spore

The prothallus is the fern gametophyte and produces sperm and eggs. Fertilisation gives a zygote that develops into the leafy sporophyte, whose sporangia produce spores. Each spore can grow into another prothallus.

Hydra develops from two principal embryonic germ layers and is described as diploblastic. A tapeworm, a flatworm, has three germ layers including mesoderm and is triploblastic. This is a structural developmental difference.

  2. lenticels
  3. air spaces
  4. guard cells
Answer and explanation

D: guard cells

Guard cells change shape as their turgor changes, opening or closing the stomatal pore. This regulates gas exchange and loss of water vapour from the leaf; the pore itself is the passage rather than the controlling cell pair.

  3. are filamentous
  4. lack chlorophyll

Fungi do not possess chlorophyll for photosynthesis and obtain organic nutrients from other sources. Having no leaves or roots is not enough to establish heterotrophy, since some photosynthetic organisms also lack those organs.

  1. glucose
  2. starch
  3. fat
  4. protein
Answer and explanation

D: protein

In the Biuret test, copper ions in alkaline solution form a violet complex with peptide bonds. A purple result therefore indicates protein or sufficiently long peptides, unlike the tests used for sugars, starch or fats.

1992 · Question 17

During alcoholic fermentation in yeast, pyruvate produced by glycolysis is converted to carbon dioxide and

  1. water
  2. glycerol
  3. ethanol
  4. citric acid
Answer and explanation

C: ethanol

During alcoholic fermentation in yeast, pyruvate is decarboxylated to acetaldehyde, releasing carbon dioxide. Acetaldehyde is then reduced to ethanol, regenerating NAD+ so glycolysis can continue.

Malpighian tubules remove nitrogenous wastes and other solutes from insect body fluid and deliver them to the gut. Subsequent water recovery helps conserve water, especially in terrestrial insects.

  1. sensory neurons
  2. efferent neurons
  3. motor neurons
  4. relay neurons
Answer and explanation

A: sensory neurons

Sensory neurons carry information from receptors toward the central nervous system. They report conditions at the body surface and within organs. Motor or efferent neurons carry commands outward, while relay neurons connect other neurons.

1992 · Question 32

In the classical model of ecological succession, which statement describes a climax community?

  1. It persists until the environment or climate changes
  2. It changes drastically from one year to the next
  3. It is the first stage in succession
  4. It is made up of the tallest trees and the smallest animals
Answer and explanation

A: It persists until the environment or climate changes

In the classical succession model, a climax community is relatively stable while its environmental conditions remain similar. Disturbance or climatic change can alter it; stability does not mean every organism or population remains unchanged.

  2. F
  3. G
  4. H
Answer and explanation

H is the top tier of the represented energy pyramid. Less energy is available at successive feeding levels because organisms use energy and release much of it as heat. The highest tier therefore represents the smallest energy supply.

  3. sand
  4. gravel

1992 · Question 39

  2. the multiplication of the malarial sporozoites in the liver
  3. the invasion of red blood cells by trophozoites
  4. the development of merozoites into gametocytes
Answer and explanation

A: parasite products released when infected red blood cells rupture, triggering inflammation

When infected red blood cells rupture, parasite products enter the blood and stimulate inflammatory signals, including cytokines. These responses contribute to fever and chills in malaria. The symptomatic blood stage differs from the initial liver infection.

1992 · Question 40

Under the standard X-linked inheritance model, a man with haemophilia has children with a woman who neither has nor carries the condition. Which statement describes their children’s genotypes?

  1. All their sons will be haemophilic
  2. All their daughters will be haemophilic
  3. All their daughters will be carriers
  4. All their sons will be carriers
Answer and explanation

C: All their daughters will be carriers

For X-linked haemophilia, the father passes his affected X chromosome to every daughter and his Y chromosome to every son. A non-carrier mother supplies an unaffected X, so all daughters are heterozygous carriers and sons do not inherit the father’s affected X. Carrier daughters can still have bleeding symptoms.

AI-reviewed · calculation checked

1992 · Question 42

If R and r are alleles at one locus, which four-cell Punnett-square result represents the expected RR × Rr cross?

  1. RR, 2Rr,rr
  2. 2RR,2rr
  3. 2RR, 2Rr
  4. 4Rr
Answer and explanation

C: 2RR, 2Rr

The RR parent contributes only R gametes, while the Rr parent contributes R or r. The expected offspring genotypes are RR and Rr in equal proportions. A four-cell Punnett square therefore gives two RR and two Rr entries.

1992 · Question 44

Which explanation can account for papaya seedlings differing from a parent tree with desirable agronomic traits?

  1. seeds are not reliable for propagating plants
  2. uncontrolled out-crossing can introduce unwanted variability
  3. vegetative propagation is the best form of reproduction for all crops
  4. seeds were not physiologically mature at harvest
Answer and explanation

B: uncontrolled out-crossing can introduce unwanted variability

Seeds formed after cross-pollination can inherit alleles from an uncontrolled pollen parent. Recombination and segregation can therefore produce offspring that differ from a desirable parent instead of faithfully cloning its traits.

  1. RNA
  2. protein
  3. DNA
  4. carbohydrate
Answer and explanation

DNA carries hereditary information in cells. Its nucleotide sequence encodes information that can be replicated and passed to daughter cells. RNA participates in gene expression, while proteins and carbohydrates have other major cellular roles.

Fleshy tissues can store water, and reduced leaves can limit the surface available for water loss. Together these features help some plants persist in dry environments, although xerophytes use many different combinations of adaptations.

Sedimentary layers can preserve remains of organisms that lived when the deposits formed. Comparing fossils in successive layers reveals changes through time and helps reconstruct the history of life.

1991 · Question 24

  1. Increased humidity
  2. Reduced temperature
  3. Reduced wind speed
  4. Reduced humidity
Answer and explanation

Lower atmospheric humidity generally steepens the water-vapour gradient between a moist leaf and the air, increasing transpiration when stomatal opening and other conditions remain unchanged. Humid air reduces that gradient.

1991 · Question 25

An open U-tube initially has equal liquid levels. Side X contains weak sugar solution and side Y strong sugar solution. A membrane between the sides allows water but not sugar to pass. After 30 minutes of osmosis, the level will

  1. rise in X only
  2. rise in Y only
  3. be the same in X and Y
  4. fall in Y
Answer and explanation

B: rise in Y only

Water moves through the membrane from the weaker sugar solution in X toward the stronger solution in Y. Sugar remains separated, so the liquid level in Y rises while that in X falls until pressure effects balance the net movement.

AI-reviewed · source checked

1991 · Question 26

In an open U-tube, water passes through a membrane from weak sugar solution X to strong sugar solution Y; the membrane does not pass sugar. What is the most specific name for this process?

  1. diffusion
  2. osmosis
  3. active diffusion
  4. osmoregulation
Answer and explanation

B: osmosis

Osmosis is net movement of water across a selectively permeable membrane down its water-potential gradient. With equal starting levels, the stronger sugar solution has lower water potential and receives water from the weaker solution.

  3. detoxication of substances
  4. storage of vitamins

Prothrombin is converted into thrombin during coagulation. Thrombin converts soluble fibrinogen into fibrin, which forms a mesh helping stabilise the blood clot. These proteins therefore participate in clotting.

Answer and explanation

D: Coelenterata

Hydra and several other cnidarians form buds that develop into new individuals. Coelenterata is the older grouping name used here for these familiar budding animals.

The gametophyte is the haploid generation that bears sex organs in bryophytes. These organs produce gametes; the dependent sporophyte instead produces spores following meiosis.

Some lizards have numerous similarly shaped teeth, the homodont pattern. Humans and rats have differentiated tooth types, while a pigeon has a toothless beak rather than homodont dentition.

Earthworm nephridia collect fluid, reclaim useful substances and eliminate metabolic wastes. They form the excretory system rather than the reproductive, respiratory or circulatory system.

Yellow fever is a viral infection transmitted by infected mosquitoes, not by houseflies. Houseflies can mechanically contaminate food or surfaces, a different transmission route from mosquito inoculation.

1990 · Question 16

Which listed plant cell type is living at maturity and has relatively thin primary walls?

  1. Collenchyma
  2. Sieve tube
  3. Xylem vessel
  4. Sclerenchyma
Answer and explanation

B: Sieve tube

Sieve-tube elements are living conducting cells with primary walls. At maturity they lose their nuclei and depend on companion cells. Collenchyma remains living but has unevenly thickened walls, while mature vessels and sclerenchyma are generally dead.

  1. activation of the chlorophyll
  2. photolysis of water
  3. reduction of carbondioxide
  4. formation of sugar
Answer and explanation

A: activation of the chlorophyll

Photosynthesis begins when pigments absorb light and their electrons become excited. This supplies energy for the light reactions; water splitting and the subsequent carbon-fixation reactions depend on this initial capture of light energy.

  3. assists in mixing digested food
  4. assists in filtering undigested food

Villi are projections of the intestinal lining that provide a large surface for nutrients to enter the body. Blood capillaries and lacteals within them carry away absorbed materials, helping maintain uptake.

1990 · Question 22

  1. plant dies immediately
  2. plant dies after two days
  3. movement of food is not affected
  4. movement of mineral salts is hardly affected
Answer and explanation

D: movement of mineral salts is hardly affected

Removing a ring of bark interrupts phloem transport of sugars. If the deeper xylem remains intact, its initial upward transport of water and dissolved mineral salts can continue, although later root starvation damages the plant.

1990 · Question 36

If an organism has a diploid chromosome number of 2n = 16, how many chromosomes are present in each normal haploid gamete?

  1. 32
  2. 16
  3. 8
  4. 4
Answer and explanation

C: 8

Normal meiosis halves the chromosome number when producing gametes. A diploid cell with 2n = 16 therefore produces haploid gametes with n = 8 chromosomes, one chromosome from each homologous pair.

1990 · Question 37

In a Mendelian monohybrid cross, pure-breeding normal-winged Drosophila (NN) are crossed with pure-breeding short-winged flies (nn). Normal wings are dominant. When the F1 flies interbreed, what are the expected F1 and F2 phenotypes?

  1. F1 all short-winged; F2 all normal-winged
  2. F1 all normal-winged; F2 all short-winged
  3. F1 all short-winged; three quarters of F2 short-winged
  4. F1 all normal-winged; three quarters of F2 normal-winged
Answer and explanation

D: F1 all normal-winged; three quarters of F2 normal-winged

Let N represent the dominant normal-wing allele and n the short-wing allele. NN × nn gives only Nn offspring with normal wings. Crossing Nn × Nn gives NN, Nn, Nn and nn, so the expected normal-winged proportion is three quarters.

Enable JavaScript to verify this question's diagram before viewing its answer.

Which stage of mitosis is represented in the cell-division diagram?

  1. prophase
  2. metaphase
  3. anaphase
  4. telophase
Answer and explanation

C: anaphase

The chromosomes are separated into two groups moving toward opposite poles. This is anaphase, when sister chromatids separate in mitosis. They are no longer lined up together at the equator and new nuclei have not yet formed.

1990 · Question 39

  2. all the red blood cells carry antigen A
  3. all the red blood cell carry antigen B
  4. there are no anti-A and anti-B antibodies in the serum
Answer and explanation

D: there are no anti-A and anti-B antibodies in the serum

In the ABO system, group AB plasma lacks anti-A and anti-B antibodies. It therefore does not attack donor red cells solely because they carry A or B antigens. Other compatibility factors still need consideration in real transfusions.

Abiotic factors are non-living physical and chemical parts of an environment. Water, temperature and soil all fit this category; organisms such as plants and reptiles are biotic components.

  3. Mouse
  4. Grasshopper

The mouse receives food from maize and from animals in the stated web. Eating both plant and animal material makes it an omnivore. The grasshopper is a plant eater, while the mantis and hawk receive animal food.

1990 · Question 44

  1. increases immediately after rain
  2. increases at the end of the rainy season
  3. decreases with increase in micro-organisms
  4. increases during the dry season
Answer and explanation

D: increases during the dry season

During a dry season, reduced freshwater input and evaporation can concentrate the salts in brackish water. Rainfall generally dilutes salts, so the seasonal effect depends on the balance of water inputs and losses.

  1. nitrogen fixing bacteria
  2. nitrifying bacteria
  3. denitrifying bacteria
  4. saprophytic bacteria
Answer and explanation

C: denitrifying bacteria

Denitrifying microorganisms reduce oxidised nitrogen compounds such as nitrate, releasing gases that can include nitrogen gas. Nitrogen fixation moves atmospheric nitrogen into biologically usable compounds, the opposite overall direction.

  2. osmosis and diffusion
  3. diffusion and active transport
  4. imbibition only
Answer and explanation

Mineral ions can enter root cells passively down suitable electrochemical gradients or by transport requiring metabolic energy. Osmosis specifically describes water movement, and imbibition is water uptake by materials.

1989 · Question 18

If a healthy plant is kept at a low positive light intensity,

  1. the rate of respiration may equal that of photosynthesis
  2. more carbon dioxide and water are taken in
  3. respiration may be halted
  4. the volume of oxygen released increases
Answer and explanation

A: the rate of respiration may equal that of photosynthesis

At the light compensation point, photosynthetic carbon uptake balances carbon dioxide release by respiration. Respiration continues in dim light, so low illumination does not stop it or necessarily increase oxygen output.

Answer and explanation

C: steapsin and trypsinogen

Pancreatic juice contains pancreatic amylase (amylopsin), pancreatic lipase (steapsin) and trypsinogen. Trypsinogen is an inactive enzyme precursor that is activated in the small intestine, limiting digestion of pancreatic tissue.

Group A serum normally contains anti-B antibodies. These bind B antigens on group B erythrocytes and cross-link the cells, causing agglutination. Matching group A or B combinations do not have that ABO incompatibility.

1989 · Question 46

A 5.0 g oven-dried soil sample weighs 4.8 g after furnace heating for 24 hours and cooling. Assuming all mass lost represents the humus being estimated, how much is present per 100 g of dry soil?

  1. 40.0 g per 100 g dry soil
  2. 4.4 g per 100 g dry soil
  3. 4.0 g per 100 g dry soil
  4. 0.4 g per 100 g dry soil
Answer and explanation

C: 4.0 g per 100 g dry soil

The mass lost is 5.0 − 4.8 = 0.2 g. Relative to the initial 5.0 g of dry soil, this is (0.2 ÷ 5.0) × 100 = 4.0 g per 100 g. This estimate assumes that the mass loss represents the organic fraction being measured.

  1. loss of fertility
  2. increase in salinity
  3. soil erosion

Irrigation water contains dissolved salts. When water evaporates or is taken up by crops and drainage does not remove enough salts, they accumulate in the root zone and can reduce crop growth.

  2. starch synthesis
  3. transport of materials

Ribosomes translate messenger RNA and join amino acids into polypeptide chains. The mRNA sequence guides amino-acid order. Ribosomes do not act as the main lipid storage or material transport structures.

1988 · Question 21

  1. upward movement of salts will cease

  4. leaves of the plant will become yellow

Phloem exports sugars from source leaves to other tissues. If phloem transport is stopped while the leaf remains healthy, sugars can accumulate above the interruption; water and mineral transport mainly uses xylem.

Turgid cells press their contents against the cell wall, producing turgor pressure. This helps support soft plant tissues. Loss of water reduces turgor and can make these tissues wilt.

1988 · Question 27

Taxis differs from a plant tropism because taxis involves

  2. it is a directional movement
  3. it is a response to multi-directional stimuli
  4. part of the organism is affected
Answer and explanation

A: directional relocation of the whole motile organism or cell

Taxis is directional movement of a whole motile organism or cell in response to a stimulus. Tropism is a directional growth response of a plant organ, such as a shoot bending towards light.

Answer and explanation

A: Scion

In grafting, the scion is joined to a compatible rootstock, which supplies the rooted base. Bulbs, rhizomes and suckers can serve as vegetative propagules without forming this scion–rootstock union.

The placenta supplies fetal gas exchange, nutrient transfer and waste removal, functions analogous to those of the

  1. lungs, kidneys and digestive system
  2. lungs, heart and nervous system
  3. liver, intestines and reproductive system
  4. intestines, heart and digestive system
Answer and explanation

A: lungs, kidneys and digestive system

The placenta allows respiratory gas exchange, transfer of nutrients and removal of fetal wastes into maternal circulation. These exchange roles resemble functions served after birth by lungs, kidneys and the digestive system.

  3. just around the root tip
  4. just below the root tip

The largest increase in root length occurs in the zone of elongation, behind the root tip. The apical meristem produces cells, which then lengthen before becoming mature specialised tissues.

  4. hydrometer

  2. Salinity
  3. Water current
  4. Turbidity
Answer and explanation

B: Salinity

Salinity is the concentration of dissolved salts and strongly distinguishes marine from freshwater conditions. Animals must maintain suitable internal salt and water balance, so tolerance of salinity limits where they can live.

  2. calcium
  3. magnesium
  4. nitrate
Answer and explanation

D: nitrate

Nitrate is soluble and is often poorly retained by negatively charged soil particles. Drainage water can therefore carry it below the root zone. Calcium and magnesium cations are more readily held at exchange sites.

  1. Gametophyte
  2. Spermatophyte
  3. Holophyte
  4. Sporophyte
Answer and explanation

D: Sporophyte

The sporophyte is the diploid multicellular generation and produces haploid spores by meiosis. Spores develop into the haploid gametophyte generation, which produces gametes. Fertilisation restores the diploid stage.

  4. Because spores are produced
Answer and explanation

Moss sperm have flagella and move through a film of water to reach the egg in an archegonium. This explains the water requirement for fertilisation. Producing or dispersing spores is a different stage of the life cycle.

  2. Secondary phloem alone
  3. Endodermis
  4. Cambium
Answer and explanation

The vascular cambium divides to produce secondary xylem towards the inside and secondary phloem towards the outside. This increases stem girth. Cork cambium also contributes to the protective outer tissues during secondary growth.

  4. To prevent ordinary roots from carrying out photosynthesis
Answer and explanation

Blocking light from the nutrient solution suppresses photosynthetic algae. Algae could otherwise use dissolved nutrients and change the culture conditions, interfering with the plant experiment. The covering need not exclude light from the plant’s leaves.

  3. wrinkled and yellow
  4. round and greenish-yellow

  2. affected mother × unaffected father
  3. carrier mother × affected father
  4. carrier mother × unaffected father
Answer and explanation

A: affected mother × affected father

An affected mother gives an affected X allele to every child; an affected father gives his affected X to every daughter. Thus all daughters receive two affected alleles.

Nitrification oxidises ammonium first to nitrite and then nitrate. Different microbial groups can perform the steps. Fixation introduces atmospheric nitrogen; denitrification can return nitrogen gas to air.

  2. Hydra
  3. Snail
  4. Cockroach

Hydra has a body organised around a central oral-to-basal axis, with tentacles around the mouth. This is the radial body plan of a cnidarian polyp. Squid and cockroaches have bilateral body plans, while adult snails show asymmetry associated with torsion.

Barbules are the small branches on the feather barbs. Hooklets on some barbules engage neighbouring barbules, linking the barbs into a continuous vane. The rachis is the central shaft, while the vane is the broader feather surface.

  4. Its normal filaments are unbranched
Answer and explanation

Spirogyra normally forms unbranched chains of cells with spiral chloroplasts. A filament can break into fragments that continue growing. Sexual reproduction involves conjugation between compatible cells rather than branching of the filament.

A grasshopper develops from egg to nymph to adult, without a pupal stage. Nymphs resemble small adults but are not yet sexually mature and usually have undeveloped wings. Butterflies, mosquitoes and houseflies pass through larval and pupal stages.

1986 · Question 9

Most fishes are described as ectothermic because their body temperature is largely

  1. Constantly low
  2. Constantly high
  3. Dependent on the temperature of their surroundings
  4. Regulated at will
Answer and explanation

C: Dependent on the temperature of their surroundings

In most fishes, heat exchange with the surrounding water strongly influences body temperature. Ectothermic does not mean always cold: a fish in warm water can have a higher temperature than one in cold water. Some fish have specialised heat-retention adaptations.

  3. Pelvic and pectoral
  4. Pectoral and dorsal
Answer and explanation

Pectoral and pelvic fins occur as left–right pairs and correspond to the two paired appendage sets. Dorsal and caudal fins are median fins. Ventral is commonly used for the pelvic fin, so it does not name a separate second pair.

1986 · Question 13

  1. On the same radius within each bundle
  2. Scattered independently throughout the ground tissue
  3. On alternate radii
  4. Only at the central pith
Answer and explanation

A: On the same radius within each bundle

In a collateral stem bundle, xylem lies towards the inside and phloem towards the outside along the same radius. In a typical root, xylem and phloem occupy alternating positions. This comparison concerns their relative arrangement, not simply the presence of either tissue.

1986 · Question 14

  1. Carbon dioxide
  2. Air
  3. Water
  4. Chlorophyll
Answer and explanation

Water is split during the light-dependent reactions, supplying electrons and releasing oxygen. Carbon dioxide supplies carbon for carbohydrate formation. Chlorophyll captures light energy but is not the substance consumed to supply the released oxygen atoms.

  3. Calcium

Each haem group contains an iron ion that binds oxygen reversibly. Haemoglobin combines these haem groups with globin protein chains to transport oxygen in red blood cells. Sodium, potassium and calcium have other physiological roles.

  1. Kwashiorkor

  3. Pellagra
  4. Scurvy
Answer and explanation

Vitamin C supports normal collagen formation. Severe deficiency causes scurvy, which can involve fragile connective tissues, bleeding gums and poor wound healing. Beriberi is associated with thiamine deficiency and pellagra with niacin deficiency.

1986 · Question 20

  1. Fats
  2. Carbohydrate
  3. Protein
  4. Reducing sugar
Answer and explanation

C: Protein

In the biuret test, copper ions in alkaline solution form a coloured complex with peptide bonds. Proteins contain many peptide bonds and can give the violet result. Fat and carbohydrate molecules do not have this protein backbone.

1986 · Question 22

Evidence of living tissues inside a mammalian tooth is found in the

  1. Gum
  2. Pulp cavity
  3. Cementum
  4. Enamel
Answer and explanation

B: Pulp cavity

The pulp cavity contains living connective tissue, blood vessels and nerves. Enamel is a hard, non-living covering of the crown. The gum surrounds the tooth, while cementum covers the root rather than containing the tooth’s central neurovascular tissue.
